# Ultra Deep Field — handoff

Last updated: 2026-11-25 (America/Phoenix). Current game version: **v845** (`GAME_V` in `docs/index.html`, and `docs/version.json`). Offline cache: **udf-v640**. (v582–v584 had left version.json at 580 and the cache at udf-v380; v586 bumps both.)

**This repo is the master copy of the game.** The claude.ai artifact is `docs/index.html` without the website-only head lines and the service-worker line at the bottom. The Android app and the Desktop launcher are being switched to load this live site. Don't overwrite `docs/index.html` with an older copy from anywhere else.

## Start here: handing off to a new assistant (written 2026-11-13, v528)

Read this section first, then the newest entries under "Pending work" below (newest at the top), then "Release checklist".

### What the game is
Ultra Deep Field: a space-empire strategy game for friends and family. One HTML file (`docs/index.html`, ~4 MB: CSS, JS, three.js r128 3D, inline art) served by GitHub Pages from `main` (a push to `main` is a deploy), installable as an app, synced through Supabase. A copy runs as a claude.ai artifact (https://claude.ai/artifact/YSphcWgn5cKuSSYNPhD8hP). The owner (account "IH IY IP IE IO N IE") designs it, paints the art and tests on a Galaxy Z Fold 6 and an iPhone.

### How changes are made (keep doing it this way)
- Every change is a Python patch script in `tools/patches/pNNN_name.py` (sometimes with a `.js` part beside it) that string-replaces exact text in `docs/index.html` and asserts each replacement matched once. Patches are applied in version order; each bumps `GAME_V` from N-1 to N.
- Release, every time: apply the patch to `docs/index.html`; bump `CACHE` in `docs/sw.js` (udf-vNNN) and `docs/version.json`; add an entry at the top of "Pending work" below (format `00000000000000000000000000. **vNNN — title** (patch; owner request). what changed, where`) and update the header lines; check the patch reproduces (`git show main:docs/index.html > r.html; python3 tools/patches/pNNN.py r.html; cmp r.html docs/index.html`); `node --check` the main script; branch `vNNN-name`, commit, `git merge --no-ff` into `main`, push both; then `python3 tools/to_artifact.py docs/index.html art.html` and publish that to the artifact URL with label "Version NNN".
- Commit trailers used so far: `Co-Authored-By:` and `Claude-Session:` lines (a new tool will have its own).
- Test before telling the owner something works: build an offline copy (`tools/dbg_build.py`, or the harness described under Testing), serve it on localhost, drive it with Playwright (`window.__dbg.ev(code)` runs code inside the game's scope), and look at screenshots at phone size (430x932, DPR 2). Headless WebGL is SwiftShader: slow but correct.

### The Advisor's voice
New Advisor lines need clips: compute each line's key (`hash(line).toString(36)`, the game's FNV-1a), add `{key, where, line}` entries with `python3 tools/voice_add.py new.json`, push, open `docs/voicelab.html` in a real browser (it runs the Kokoro model on the GPU), "Load model" then "Make only the missing lines"; it downloads a zip of WAVs. Convert each with `ffmpeg -i k.wav -af loudnorm=I=-19:TP=-1.5 -ar 32000 -ac 1 -b:a 56k docs/voice/k.mp3` and set `index.json[k]=1`. The game plays a line if its key is in `docs/voice/index.json`.

### Server
Supabase project `slubtpfnimxtrfcozvhk`. Edge Function `settle_fight` (`supabase/functions/settle_fight/`) re-runs boss fights with `sim.js`, the battle simulator cut from the game by `tools/sim_bundle.py` (`THREE_JS=<three.min.js> python3 tools/sim_bundle.py`). The deployed function imports `sim.js` from jsDelivr pinned to a commit (now e7785259…, game v515, battle rules v6); after any change to `simulate`, unit stats (AL/SH/DF), CPROF or RF, rebuild `sim.js`, push, and redeploy with the import pinned to the new commit. It only logs agreement in `fight_log` (phase 2); nothing depends on it yet.

### Rules the owner set (keep them)
- Never upload, publish or print the Android keystore, `keystore-password.txt` or `netlify-token.txt`. Never commit `seed.json` (player data).
- New lore (story text, Archives/Bestiary records) needs the owner's approval before it goes in.
- Don't repeat the same Advisor line over and over; he dislikes repetitive speech.
- Find problems yourself and check fixes the way a player sees them (screenshots at phone size), and fix things once.
- He sends art as images in chat; his paintings are in `docs/art/` (`art/mon/` for monsters and creatures, `art/arch/` for the Archives).

### State right now (v737, 2026-11-23): read this before the v528 notes below
- **Android app (Google Play closed test).** The app is a plain WebView wrapper, not a TWA (Testers Community rejected the TWA). Source is in `android/` on `main`: Java `MainActivity` (splash screen, offline page, `UDFNative` bridge for `saveFile` blob downloads and `retry`, auto-reload when the network returns, `onRenderProcessGone` recovery, user agent suffix ` UDFApp/1.0.1`), compileSdk/targetSdk 36, minSdk 24, **versionCode 4 / versionName 1.0.1**, portrait and touchscreen declared `required="false"`, adaptive icon (galaxy in a cube, background `#06142A`, 9% inset). The game itself is the live site, so game releases need no new app build.
- **Building the app.** `.github/workflows/android.yml` builds unsigned AAB/APK and pushes them to branch `android-out`. `android-smoke.yml` with `.github/smoke.sh` runs an emulator smoke test and pushes results to `android-smoke`. Signing happens on the owner's PC VM (`~/signer/sign.sh`, alias `my-key-alias`). The keystore and its password never leave that machine. Signed builds are in the owner's `Downloads/UDF-PlayStore-package/webview-build/` (newest `UDF-1.0.1-build4/`). Each new upload needs a higher versionCode.
- **Play testing.** Closed-test release 4 (1.0.1) is live. Testers Community: credit spent, 16-day run (status was "Starting shortly", day 0, on 2026-11-22). Production access can be applied for after the 14+ days with enough testers.
- **Performance work (v728–v735).** Live perf telemetry goes to Supabase `docs` (`col='feedback'`, `kind='perf'`: fields `vw`, `cpu`, `map`, `gl`, `bench`, `frz`). Battles moved their glows, beams and backdrop onto WebGL layers (`.bgu` under, the 2D `#bcv` in the middle, `.bgl` over with `plus-lighter`; code from `tools/patches/p731_gl.js`/`p732_gl.js`, functions `bglBegin`… `bglFree`; `?gl2d` forces the old 2D path). Battle fps on the owner's Fold went 11 → 27 → about 59 by the numbers. The galaxy draws planets as instanced batches.
- **Open: battle still feels choppy to the owner** (his last answer: "Choppy / stutters"). Known candidates: a ~1.2 s freeze at battle start while `playBattle` re-runs the simulation; ships are still drawn on the 2D canvas; the label overlay. Check the newest `kind='perf'` rows (`frz`, `ft`) from his phone before changing anything.
- **v736–v737: Cube Destroyer (`worldbreaker`) look.** Its dish is a real opening (the hull, trench and collar shaders discard around the dish axis), and the Glyph Holo livery no longer makes it see-through (`userData.solidHolo` / `keepOBC`, honoured by both `finMat` and `livMat`). Sparkle glints stay inside the ship's outline. Check new ship finishes with the studio-art test (`twb.js`, `twbg.js` in the harness) and the Glyph Holo accent.
- **Repo remote moved** to `https://github.com/gunhypechannel/Ultradeepfield.git` (the old URL still redirects; update `origin` when convenient).
- **Artifact copy:** `python3 tools/to_artifact.py docs/index.html <out.html>` then publish to https://claude.ai/artifact/YSphcWgn5cKuSSYNPhD8hP with the `pk/season.json`, `pk/arch.json`, `pk/misc.json`, `pk/crc.json` files beside it.

Cause: the faction question (`G.askFac`) is spent on the boot save; when the account's cloud save is then taken (sign-in / new install), nothing re-asked, so a save that had stopped during the opening film loaded into a base with no faction, no prologue and no guidance. Serpent, Free view, 400 frames: frames moving it more than 0.15 of the screen 131 -> 0, largest per-frame screen move 1.33 -> 0.045, camera per-frame 2.4 -> 0.1 units; Sentinel: body within 0.2 of its spot, no shove.

- **v821 (2026-11-27): the Cube Destroyer holds its post again** (`tools/patches/p821_wire.py`). Owner video: "Cube destroyer is moving once again" (a replay against a Cube boss; the Destroyer crossed the field). Cause: the v531/v659 post was inside `if (!b.superSide)`, so in every battle with a boss side (the Cube's boss forms, super and world bosses) the Destroyer floated with the layout; and its edge (side-on or top/bottom, and which end) was recomputed every frame from both fleets' average positions, so it could flip to the other edge mid-battle. Now the post holds unless the Destroyer is itself on the boss side (`b.superSide === q.side`), and the edge is decided once per battle (`b.cdA[i].v`, `.e`). Harness replay (Destroyer and 380 ships against 4 Cubes, 3 Spheres, 30 Drones, `omega` so `superSide` "d"): before, mid-field and wandering 25 px; after, held at its edge within 7 px after the first placement. The owner's longer swing (an edge flip) did not reproduce in the short test fight.

CSS: Sanctuary turntables (`.xsspin`) stand still until a pointer or finger is on the creature, then turn at half speed (9.6 s); the decorative rays and rings (Sanctuary, reveal, forge, celebration, premium rings, Galaxy War, rewards, rank avatars, Void Pass) turn 2.5x slower, their flickers and pops unchanged. Harness: base view loads, `MOT` live, street flow offset 0, no errors.
- **v823 (2026-11-27): the Eclipse Harbinger, more of it** (`tools/patches/p823_wire.py` + `p823_harb.js`). Owner: "Need more Eclipse Harbinger lines please, this ship needs to be super important ... It stages the Harbinger's own main gun (`b.hc823` counts per ship); the volley's damage lands as the cage closes (1.5 s), so results and balance are unchanged. Lines (`HARB_L`, text; voice when ElevenLabs credits allow, asking first): sending it out (2, a reply to the tap, at most every 10 min; against another commander the long-range sighting line), after a battle it fought (2 wins, 1 loss, replacing the usual remark), on the base (2, once a day, queued), awakened within 3 days of an Eclipse (once a day, Important), bound (once a day). Harness: a Harbinger and 40 ships against 500: the Cage on its 4th volley, every stage screenshotted (pylons, cage, inward beams, eclipse, burst), no errors; a battle report with the Harbinger picks its own line.
- **v824 (2026-11-27): the base on phones and tablets, lighter and steadier** (`tools/patches/p824_wire.py`, `tools/glb/simplify.js`). Owner: "the app still feels a little laggy especially in base view ... 60 fps stable", "Do that". The owner's own performance reports (Android app UDFApp/1.0.1, Mali-G68 tablet, v822): the base at 9-17 fps, frames ~60 ms at pixel ratio 1 (adaptRes had already given up all the resolution it could), ~2 million triangles on screen, CPU 18-44 ms a frame, freezes of 1.2-4.7 s: geometry and script bound, not pixel bound; spin speed was never the cost. Harness (8-core Android tablet, base): 684k triangles, of which the colonists ~300k (46 figures of ~3,000, a few pixels tall) and the ground grid 51k. Now on MOBILE: colonist geometry at 35% of its segments (`FSG`, min 5; 38 counts) and at most 22 colonists (was 46); the ground grid 96x96 (was 160x160); `fpsCap()` = the player's cap, else `G.autoCap`: a phone or tablet over 23 ms a frame for ~90 frames at its lowest resolution locks to a steady 30 fps (crumb "auto 30 fps lock"), lifting every 2 minutes to retry. Same harness: 339k triangles (-50%), zoomed in the figures still read. Not done: the building GLBs (20-60k each; the owner's base adds Sanctuary, Lab, Fusion, Nanite) could not be decimated: they are almost all unwelded, non-manifold (Lab: 66,584 vertices for 53,831 triangles) and meshoptimizer locks such edges; the sloppy simplifier is not in the bundled meshopt and npm could not be reached. `tools/glb/simplify.js` (weld + simplify + per-island remap) is kept for when it can.
- **v825 (2026-11-27): the circling fleet is gone, the Harbinger opens with its Cage, and the base is lighter on phones** (`tools/patches/p825_wire.py`, `tools/glb/cluster2.js`, `tools/glb/tex1024.py`, `tools/glb/cluster.js`, 19 files in `docs/models/m/`). Owner: "remove the fleet animation when an attack lands in map view a wedge formation fleet circles the target, it uses outdated ship models and proportion scaling, i want it fixed or removed"; "do all of it"; "watched a battle report a few times and never seen harbingers new attacks?". (1) The circling wedge was your own fleet parked at its target (fleetPath park) orbiting every 7.9 s in the old travelling-fleet model on top of the battle's own flagship and escort (v815): while a battle plays at that target it is hidden; other parked fleets (not mining or mustering) orbit at the muster pace (.00011, was .0008). (2) The Eclipse Cage (v823) came only on the Harbinger's 4th main volley; the owner's fights are short (their Void Shark hunt r4948: 7.75 s, the Harbinger fired only its Neural Swarm, never its main gun), so it never appeared. Now it is also staged right before the Harbinger's first shot of any kind (visual only; damage unchanged), then every 4th main volley as before. It was also sized by the Harbinger's own on-screen size, so far from the camera it closed ~20 px wide under its target: it now spans the target (min ~120 px, max ~420 px). (3) Mobile building models: my own crack-free vertex clusterer (one position grid for the whole mesh so texture seams move together; detached pieces smaller than 4 cells keep every vertex). Render-checked each against the original from two angles: 13 at 40% (radar, fusion, nanite, metalMine, robotics, gasSynth, lunarBase, gasTank, metalStore, crystalStore, repairYard, fortress, solar), 5 at 60% where 40% roughened thin parts (lab's hologram dome, crystalMine frame, shipyard arm, allyDepot, phalanx dish): 459k -> 211k triangles. Sanctuary failed (its glass dome shatters) and stays full. Their main colour textures 1536 -> 1024 on mobile (19 models incl. terraformer; no visible change at base size; ~2.25x less to upload, the likely cause of the 1-5 s freezes). Mobile-only fingerprints `GLB_VM`. (4) Base per-frame work: buildings off screen skip their (clock-driven) machinery animation; colonists step at 30 Hz on mobile; half the pebbles on mobile; pebbles and far boulders cast no shadows there. Emulated Android tablet (software GL, direction only): base render 3.6 s -> 0.75 s a frame, base script 103 -> 19 ms a frame. Originals of the mobile GLBs are the desktop ones in `docs/models/` plus git history.
- **v826 (2026-11-27): the Celestial Serpent's face** (`tools/patches/p826_wire.py`, `tools/sculpt/mon3_sculpt.py`, `docs/art/mon/m/cserp.bin`). Owner: "celestial serpant model still broken, whats going on with its face". Cause: since v819 the sculpt's skull and jaw were empty. `lathe_x` takes its profile snout first (it reverses the list), and the serpent's head listed it back to front, so `np.interp` got descending x and the end caps cut everything; the horns, frill, eyes, teeth and the mouth glow floated with no head under them. Now: profiles reversed, the head held more level (-.2, was -.42 nose-down), a broader blunt snout, the jaw nearly closed (.09 rad, was .3) with the fangs along the mouth line, larger deep-violet eyes set proud of the skull, gold plates down the crown and a gold snout tip, as in the painting. Checked in the game's own material and swim wave from the side, above, close on the head and head-on. 27k triangles, 0.41 MB as before. No other sculpt listed a lathe profile backwards.
- **v827 (2026-11-27): the Celestial Serpent's face, refined after the painting** (`tools/patches/p827_wire.py`, `tools/sculpt/mon3_sculpt.py`, `docs/art/mon/m/cserp.bin`). Owner: "how can we refine and improve the mouth and face design even further?" then "lets build it". The head is rebuilt as a viper's: a rounded wedge (`wedge`, flat crown, angled sides, broad cheeks) plated in gold (`cap`) with dark seams and a centre ridge (seams banded to the crown's skin so they never cut the horns); violet eyes with black slit pupils under jutting gold brows; gold lips over a lit mouth slit (`MOUTH` palate and tongue) with a snarl lifting the lip at the corner; small teeth hung outside the lower jaw and long front fangs; a separate lower jaw (`Jt`/`Jb`) with a gold chin plate, lower teeth, cheek plates, nostrils; less metallic skin (the face went grey head-on). The jaw opens in the swim wave: vertices below the mouth line and inside the upper teeth (exact head width, piecewise, in the shader) turn about the hinge up to ~16 degrees for ~2 s every ~10 s on the map and once per 6 s battle loop (checked in the baked battle frames ~f0 closed, ~f10 open). The lower lip sits at .72 of the head width and the upper teeth at .97 + .03 so no surface spans the cut (only the mouth corner stretches). Voxel .03; 35k triangles, 0.53 MB (was 27k, 0.41 MB).
- **v828 (2026-11-27): the Celestial Serpent's attacks and its elemental fire** (`tools/patches/p828_wire.py`). Owner: "add all that and any kind of elemental glow effects make it look unique, the other monsters look great". Attack poses: 12 more baked battle frames (`~f100+`: six swim phases x jaw half / full open; `monBatPose` turns frame ids >= 100 into a phase and an `atk` constant, the wave opens the jaw to ~36 degrees at full and the head's emission flares up to x3.2 before `aomap_fragment`; the map injects `atk = 0`). `serpJaw` times it: Coil Strike charges jaw and eyes to the strike, gapes at the bite, closes; Constrict lunges open, holds half open through the squeeze, gapes at the crush; every other shot (`b.serpV`) snaps it half open. While it attacks `serpFrame` settles its swim phase onto the nearest of the six poses and crossfades swim -> half -> full. `serpFx` traces each frame's picture once (`serpSpine`: its back, midline and mouth) and draws violet spirit flames leaning back along its back, gold and violet embers rising off the body, a violet mouth glow that swells as it opens, and a gout of violet fire from the mouth on each strike (fewer on low FX). Its shots, lances, beams and muzzle flashes now leave its mouth (`b.serpMo`, also for squads drawn at its spot), and the Coil Strike trail follows its head, thinner: the old trail sat on the body's middle and piled into a white blob. Checked frame by frame in a test fight (coil charge, bite, close; Constrict crush) and on the map.
- **v829 (2026-11-27): the Advisor log reads back every voiced line, at once** (`tools/patches/p829_wire.py`). Loader: `WB_SC` / `WB_GEO` / `wbSculpt(b)` (all parts of a boss fetched together; exposed on `window` because BOSS3D is its own script), `wbScMat()` (a per-build copy of the sculpt material: each boss renderer sets its own envMap), `wbSculptSwap(b)` (drops the map's built model and resets `WBR.key` so both rebuild). - **v748 (2026-11-24): the Resurrection War at 10,000 commanders** (owner: "finish it all"; `tools/patches/p748_wire.py` + `p748_scale.js`, `docs/udf-supabase.js` `window.udfCnt`, server `supabase/migrations/v748_cnt_scale.sql`). Before, every fight and every war-point flush took a lock on `event/cnt`, read the whole record, changed it and wrote it back (compare-and-set), and the record carried every commander's points (`con`, `cday`) and damage (`dmg`, `fin.dmg/hits`): ~1 MB at 10,000 commanders, sent old+new to every open game on every write (too big for a live message, so every game re-read it each time), with the lock serialising everyone behind network round trips. Now, on the Supabase build: `cnt_apply(path, k, ops)` applies war points (`con`), network hits (`hit`: relay down, Ark destroyed, commander transfer or death, capacity spent, the 12 h relay rebuild) and finale fights (`fin`: the timers of `cntFinNorm` ported as `cnt_fin_norm`, phases, anchors -> p3, hangars/conduits -> p4, transmitter cut, reactor -> win/part, convoys, escorts) in one short transaction with the same rules, and returns the record, per-op `{ev, seg, counted}` and this commander's own numbers (`cnt_me_of`). Points live in `cnt_con` (one row per commander, 300/day cap server-side), damage shares in `cnt_share`; the record keeps only shared state plus `n`, `top` (five) and each Ark's `tot`, flagged `v2`. pg_cron folds points into it every 15 s (`cnt_roll`) and sends the newest record to every game every 3 s when it changed (`cnt_bcast`; the per-write broadcast skips v2 war records). `d_cnt_guard2` refuses any direct write to a v2 record (only the server functions change it); the v746 rules still apply. A v1 record is converted on the first server write (its maps moved into the tables). The game reads a v2 record through `cntV2Fill` (fills its own maps and the top five, so the rest of the code is unchanged), counts commanders from `n`, Ark shares from `tot`, the finale's top ten from `t10`; `cntMeLoad` refreshes its own numbers (War Command, target sheets, at most every 20 s). The server path is used only after the server has answered once (`cntProbe`, `cntSrvW`: a fight waits for the answer); otherwise, and in the claude.ai artifact, the old path runs unchanged. If the target fell to someone else while the fleet flew, it returns with nothing lost. Also: each finale preview now gets a fresh record (`cntKey` = `prev` + start time; the second preview used to open on the first one's finished finale). Verified: 30 rule cases against a local Postgres 16 with the migration (caps, blocked targets, transfer/death, eternal commander, every finale phase, cascade regrowth, timer failures, v1 conversion, owner-only preview, direct writes refused); the game's own resolve code end to end against it (relay hits, flush, War Command, four finale strikes, Champion tier); fallback with the functions missing; old offline path (tnet). Load test (pgbench, 2 CPUs, 10,000 commanders seeded): war points 1,760/s at 100 at once (needed ~110/s), finale fights 250/s (500 results/s) with every one counted, mixed 950/s with zero failures; a broadcast every 3 s of ~1.4 KB. Server migration applied to the live project 2026-11-24 (owner: "approve it all") and tested there inside a rolled-back transaction (daily cap, relay down, shielded Ark refused, commander transfer, finale closed before start / anchor hit, direct write refused, non-owner preview refused, roll-up, broadcast mark); two fixes found live and applied: the v746 guard's "war points grew" check skips v2 records (the server totals them), and the server-only permission (`udf.cnt_rpc`) is cleared at the end of each call. Cron runs take 2-9 ms; `cron_log_trim` keeps one day of run records. The Glyph Wheel's shared pot (`supabase/v622_wheel_pool.sql`, never applied since v622) was applied 2026-11-24 and tested (spin adds, a jackpot takes half, state lists the win; rolled back). Offline cache `udf-v543`.

- **v743 (2026-11-24): the whole Continuum fleet is sculpted** (owner: "Continue" on the sculpting pipeline). - **v742 (2026-11-24): the sculpting pipeline; the Resurrection Ark is sculpted** (owner: "i want to do the sculpting pipeline ... to be as visually accurate" to the concept art). `tools/sculpt/`: `sdf.py` (signed-distance primitives incl. `hull` armour plates and box-section `ring`s, smooth unions, carves that label grooves with their own material, marching cubes via scikit-image, normals from the field gradient, per-vertex albedo/roughness/emission/metalness from the nearest part, baked AO), one model script per class (`cnt_ark.py <outdir> [voxel]`, ~2 min at .045), `pack.js <dir> <tris> <out.bin> [maxErr]` (meshoptimizer `simplifyWithAttributes`, vendored as `mo.js`; writes the CNT2 binary: quantized positions, i8 normals, u8 albedo (sqrt) + roughness, u8 emission + metalness, u16/u32 indices). In the game (`tools/patches/p742_wire.py` + `p742_sculpt.js`): `buildCnt` starts `cntSculptLoad()` on first use; once `art/rwar/m/<k>.bin` arrives (`CNT_GEO[k]`) it returns `cntSculpted(k)` (scale `CNT_SC[k].s` into the procedural frame, engine mouths listed there) and `cntSculptSwap` drops cached studio art (`STU.cache`), `MMERGE`, `G3.monMaster` and the map models of that class so they rebuild; until then, or if the file fails, the procedural hull stands in. `cntMeshMat` damps direct specular on dark albedo (the studio's blue rim light had turned black metal pale). `mergeGroup` keeps a sculpted mesh whole (it carries its own attributes); `monAnimate` skips Continuum species with no swim wave (they used to get a shader with `undefined` in it). Ark: 43k tris, 1.3 MB. Checked in the studio art (side and hero) and the map cinematic. Next: the other nine classes the same way. Offline cache `udf-v538`.

- **v740 (2026-11-24): The Resurrection War, phases 1-3 (switched off for players)** (`tools/patches/p740_wire.py` + `p740_cnt_a.js` (data, clock, map targets, state) + `p740_cnt_b.js` (network fights, War Command, map layer, Battle Lab, the Continuum's 3D hulls) + `p740_cnt.css`; owner: "build it all"; plan in the Claude Doc "The Resurrection War: development plan"). Prefix `cnt`/`CNT_` (the Continuum). Runs in every Season of the Ruin (`seaCyc 7`, first 16 Aug 2027), seven stages over 28 days (`CNT_STG`), stage 7 the finale on the closing Saturday 6 pm Phoenix (`CNT_FIN`, not built yet). **Switch**: players see nothing until `docs/version.json` carries `"f": {"cnt": 1}` (`G.flags`, read by the version check); the owner previews any stage (`S.cntPrev`, War Command or Battle Lab). Units `cnint cncor cnexe cnfab cnasm cndre cnrel cnark cnprime cneter cncom` (AL, CPROF, MIM_HULL; models `buildCnt`). Map: 30 patrols (`cnpat`), 24 platforms (`cnplat`), and the network in `MIM_BOSS` with `cnt` set: 4 Arks (`ka0-3`, 160 sections), 12 relays (`krIJ`, 60, on their Ark's orbit), 8 commanders (`kc0-7`, 40), the Eternal Commander (`ke0`, 200, stage 6). Rules: relays attackable from stage 4, Arks from 5 and only with all three relays down; a commander whose sections run out is rebuilt 6 h later while its Ark lives, has capacity (6) and a relay up, else gone for good; relays rebuild 12 h after falling unless their Ark is dead; a dead Ark weakens its region's garrisons by a quarter. Network targets ignore fog. Shared doc `event/cnt` (`event/cnt_prev` for previews) under the Carcinid lock + compare-and-set; war progress `cntWar`. Machine tactics applied in every Continuum fight and noted (`CNT_TAC`, `cntMods`). Orders per stage incl. noncombat (decrypt a patrol's signal, scan a relay by opening its page), war milestones 25/50/75/100%, the Arkbreaker reward (once per Ark, claim key first). Map layer `cntMapTick`: red sectors, crimson relay-to-Ark lines. Verified in the harness: relays fall, an Ark loses its shield and dies, its Arkbreaker reward pays once, a commander is rebuilt (capacity 6 -> 5) and another dies for good when its Ark is gone. **Not built yet**: the Extinction Protocol finale, the forge/shop/reward track and cosmetics, cinematics, Archive records, Advisor lines, joint strikes on network targets, the Edge Function for resurrection rules. All Continuum lore and names are drafts awaiting the owner's approval.

- **v738 (2026-11-23): battles on a computer screen** (owner, with a video of a battle on a monitor: "the battle looks messy and cramped"). Ship sizes in `drawBattle` were all fractions of the field's width, so a landscape window blew every hull up (at 1600x900 the Cube Destroyer was ~690 px across and covered the field). Each squadron's scale now takes `wdk = clamp(H * .8 / W, .4, 1)` (1 on phones in portrait, ~.45 on a 16:9 monitor), which shrinks ships and the effects sized from them. `cardGrade` window strips are masked to the solid hull (alpha > 200), not the bloom halo, so they no longer run out past a hull's edges; the Cube Destroyer skips `cardGrade` (straight strips across a sphere) and bakes at 1024 px on computers so it stays sharp. Checked at 1600x900 and 430x932 (phone unchanged). Offline cache `udf-v534`.
- **v737 (2026-11-23): the Cube Destroyer in Glyph Holo is solid** (owner, with a photo: "The cube destroyer still has a distorted and overlapped look that extends past its edges"). Titans wear `livMat`, not `finMat`, so the v736 dish opening was dropped and the Glyph Holo livery made the sphere see-through: five translucent spheres in one formation read as one tangled ghost, with runes across the dish. A hull material marked `userData.solidHolo` (the worldbreaker hull, trench and collar) now stays opaque in both `finMat` and `livMat`, darkening its "glass" parts toward black instead of showing what is behind (`outgoingLight * lvAl`); a material marked `keepOBC` keeps its own `onBeforeCompile` (the dish cut) under the finish. `finSpark` glints shrink or drop so their crosses never stick out past the silhouette. Checked in the studio art (Glyph Holo and plain). Offline cache `udf-v533`.
- **v736 (2026-11-23): the Cube Destroyer's dish is a real opening** (owner, with a photo of the dish showing the hull's plating across it: "Cube destroyer looks like it has an overlapping texture. Can we refine it better?"). In `buildShip` (`worldbreaker`) the dish disc sat at 0.93 R, just under the sphere's skin, so the plating, its window lights and the equatorial trench were drawn across it. The hull and trench materials now discard pixels within 0.43 rad of the dish axis (`onBeforeCompile`, object-space position; `customProgramCacheKey` per axis), the dish is recessed to 0.865 R inside a dark open collar, a red lip torus sits exactly on the cut, the inner rings and the emitter moved onto the recessed dish. Checked in the studio art (plain and own-fleet accents). Offline cache `udf-v532`.
- **v735 (2026-11-23): no garbage from the GPU battle layer** (owner: battles feel "Choppy / stutters"). `bglQuad` wrote every glow into new JS arrays and `bglDrawBatch` made a new Float32Array per sprite per frame; each sprite now keeps one growing Float32Array reused every frame (`{a, n}`), so the layer adds no garbage-collection pauses. Freeing a battle's GPU layers no longer logs "battle GPU layer lost" (the crash report blamed it after a normal close). v733 data: the battle itself ran smooth (2 frames over 34 ms in 56 s); the visible hitches were a 1.2 s freeze as a battle opens (the replay is re-simulated on the main thread in `playBattle`) and the tier see-saw fixed in v734. Offline cache `udf-v531`.
- **v734 (2026-11-23): battles hold 60 without see-sawing** (owner: "Battle report still slow and laggy with high graphics"). The v733 reports from the owner's Fold app: battle 54-59 fps (script 7.5-8 ms a frame, from 35 ms on v729), galaxy 52 fps (frame 19 ms: worlds 5.4, GPU submit 5.3, stations 3.5, labels 2.7). What still felt laggy: on a 120 Hz screen a fast battle earned its way back to tier 0 after 5 s, whose sunlit rims, glints and full-screen soft-light pass (a 2D self-copy) made the next seconds slow, then it stepped back up: a stutter every few seconds. Phones now never climb back below tier 1, the soft-light pass is skipped on phones in GPU mode, and the step-down starts below ~52 fps (`b.ft > 19`, was 22). Offline cache `udf-v530`.
- **v733 (2026-11-23): the galaxy's near-world pass runs every 88 ms when zoomed out** (owner: "finish"). `fpGap` in `draw3D`: 44 ms in planet view, with close-up worlds on screen (`W.dets`) or in a cinematic; 88 ms otherwise, where an orbit moves well under a pixel between passes. This was the largest part of a galaxy frame on the owner's Fold (`worlds`, 10-12 ms). Still open for 60 fps: battle ships are drawn on the 2D canvas between far/near haze, wrecks and smoke, so moving them to the GPU means a full WebGL battle renderer (not a layer swap); galaxy labels/2D overlay (~3.7 ms, a full-screen 2D canvas the WebView redraws and uploads every frame) and stations/fields (~6 ms, the obstacle grid `obGrid` is rebuilt every frame and monsters add themselves to it, so it can't simply be throttled). Offline cache `udf-v529`.
- **v732 (2026-11-23): battle backdrop and every glow on the GPU; empty planet batches not drawn** (owner: "Yes finish up"). The v731 module is replaced by `tools/patches/p732_gl.js` (wired by `p732_wire.py`): the battle is three layers. `.bgu` (WebGL, under `#bcv`) draws the backdrop `b.bg` as one textured quad moved with the camera (uploaded again only when `b.bgV` changes); `#bcv` is cleared each frame instead of copying the backdrop; `.bgl` (WebGL, over, `plus-lighter`) gets the light layer (the 2D part of `b.fxc` is uploaded only when something non-glow was drawn there: `b.fxDirty`), every glow sprite drawn in "lighter" mode on `#bcv` or the light layer (`bglHook` overrides `drawImage` on those two contexts: cached sprites from `GSPR`/`RSPR`, no clip, no shadow, no filter -> `bglQuad` batches them per texture), the bloom, and the screen flash (`b.glFlash`, a uniform). `bglBegin` (start of `drawBattle`) / `bglLight` (where the light layer was composited) / `bglFlush` (end of `drawBattle`). GPU mode is off in low effects (`lo`), without WebGL or plus-lighter, after a lost context (`b.glFail`) or with `?gl2d`, and the old 2D drawing runs. Glows that sat under a hull now add over it. The clip recorder stacks `.bgu`, `#bcv`, then `.bgl` in "lighter". Headless, 260-ship battle at 1.5x: 14 ms a frame vs 42 ms on the 2D path. Galaxy: an `InstancedMesh` with `count` 0 still cost three.js a draw call, so each planet batch is now `visible` only when it has instances (64 batches -> 24 drawn on a fresh map). Offline cache `udf-v528`.
- **Plan after v731 (owner chose "Option 1: keep the web game, put everything on the graphics chip")**: measured on the owner's Fold app: battle 11 -> 27 fps with v731 (script 35 -> 16 ms a frame, frame interval ~37 ms); `bench` 4.8 ms = same as the dev container, so the app is not CPU-throttled and the rest is drawing/compositing of 2D canvases (the WebView rasters 2D without the GPU and re-uploads them every frame). Next: (1) battle part 2, a WebGL battle renderer: backdrop as a GPU texture, ships as batched textured quads from the baked unit art, trails/beams as GPU lines, HUD text kept on a small 2D layer; (2) galaxy map: ~550 draw calls -> merge/instance, ~950k triangles -> lighter far spheres, the 44 ms full world pass (10-12 ms) spread over frames, labels (3.7 ms) only redrawn when the camera moves.
- **v731 (2026-11-23): the battle's light layer and bloom run on the GPU** (owner: "How can we make our game run directly on phones graphic chip?" ... "Yes start 1"). Profiling a battle frame with the 2D canvas drawn in software (as the Android WebView does) showed the glow sprites themselves were cheap; the cost was the full-screen work after them: the bloom's enlargement to full size (~37 ms), laying the light layer over the field (~9 ms) and the bloom's first shrink (~7 ms). `tools/patches/p731_gl.js`: `bglLight` uploads the light layer (`b.fxc`) to WebGL once a frame, shrinks it 4x and 16x (4-tap box), blurs each level (9-tap separable Gaussian), and writes light x exposure + bloom to its own canvas `.bgl` over `#bcv`, blended by the compositor with `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` (the same sum as the old "lighter" draw). Falls back to the old 2D path without WebGL or plus-lighter, after a lost context (`b.glFail`), or with `?gl2d`. The clip recorder adds `.bgl` with "lighter"; `battleFree` loses the context. Headless test, 707x739 at 1.5x, 260-ship battle: 13 ms a frame vs 43 ms on the 2D path, same look. Offline cache `udf-v527`.
- **v729 (2026-11-23): alerts tidy + galaxy frame breakdown** (owner, with a Fold screenshot of four alerts stacked over each other: "is there anyway we can clean up and optimize the view when alerts come in?"). `toast()` drops a plain line when the same job's card is up (`toastKey` normalises "X reached Lv.N" / "Research complete: X" / "finished" / "joined the garrison"), `remind()` removes the plain twin and keeps one card per kind; plain toasts max 2. While the Advisor hologram is on, any toast under her portrait or speech box gets `.advhide` (a 200 ms check) and returns after. The base log (`.ticker`) is padded right of the Feedback button when they overlap (`--tkPL`, set in the v628 `--fbB` interval; fixed elements have no offsetParent, so visibility is read from computed style). Perf: `MAPPROF` is always on (`MAPPROF.hud` is the old on-screen switch) and `pfSave` puts its averages (`map`) and the renderer's draw calls, triangles, programs and visible objects (`gl`) in the perf report. The v728 numbers from the owner's Fold app: galaxy 40 fps with 20 ms of script per frame (67% of frames over 12 ms), so the map is script-bound there. Offline cache `udf-v525`.
- **v728 (2026-11-23): frame reports now carry script time per frame** (owner: "The game is super laggy on the app, what can we do to make sure its holding 60fps stable"). `frame()` is wrapped (`frameIn` holds the old body) and `pfCpu` adds `[frames, ms, frames over 12 ms]` per screen to the perf report's new `cpu` field (feedback docs, kind "perf"). Compare `cpu` ms/frame with the `vw` interval: script-bound if they are close, drawing/GPU/power-limited if the interval is much longer. Data before it: the owner's Fold 6 in the WebView app (UA `wv ... UDFApp`, GPU string "Adreno (TM) 750" without ANGLE) ran the galaxy at 29-35 fps at pixel ratio 1.31 (hz read 60); the same Fold in Chrome desktop mode at 3.5x got 27 fps on the galaxy, so resolution is not the limit there. Offline cache `udf-v524`.
- **v727 (2026-11-22): the Android app is now a WebView app, not a PWABuilder TWA** (owner: "Start 1 and resubmit", after Testers Community refused TWA apps). New `android/` Gradle project (package `com.udf.ultradeepfield` unchanged, versionCode 4 / 1.0.1 (2 discarded before review; 3 went live to testers 2026-11-22 19:40 with the old spiral icon from the PWABuilder build; 4 has the current galaxy-in-a-cube icon made from docs/icons, adaptive foreground = icon-maskable-512 inset 9%), minSdk 24, target 36): one WebView on the game URL with a splash screen, an offline screen with Retry that also reloads by itself when the connection returns, recovery from a renderer crash, the back button, file import and file saving (blob downloads go to Downloads through `UDFNative.saveFile`). It adds ` UDFApp/1.0.1` to the user agent; the site now treats that as installed (`STANDALONE` in index.html, udf-install.js, udf-supabase.js), so the "Get the app" button and the Add-to-Home tip stay hidden in the app. Built unsigned by `.github/workflows/android.yml` (pushes to branch `android-out`), smoke-tested on an emulator by `android-smoke.yml` (branch `android-smoke`), then signed on the owner's PC with the PWABuilder upload key (`signing.keystore`, which never leaves that PC). Google sign-in is off on this server, so no OAuth handling was needed; if it is switched on, Google blocks its login inside a WebView and the app will need a Custom Tab hand-off. Web push does not work in a WebView. Portrait and touchscreen are declared optional (Play blocked the release otherwise). Signed AAB/APK: `Downloads/UDF-PlayStore-package/webview-build/UDF-1.0.1-build4/` on the owner's PC. Sent to Google review on the closed Alpha track 2026-11-22 and submitted to Testers Community (Starter credit spent). Offline cache `udf-v523`.

Its swarm shader turned each vertex by an angle that grew with radius, so wasps smeared into orange shards over time: now the swarm turns rigidly and bobs by angle. Map monsters drift `RX 1.4 × RZ .9` round their spot (was 7.5 × 5, ~15 slots wide) and the tap target is the drawn body, not the loop centre. On iOS, opening a battle replay calls `worldDrop("gal"/"col", "before a battle")`, so a map or base kept alive behind another screen (e.g. Mail) is released; the world on screen is still kept (worldDrop never drops it). Still open: v714 crash reports show kills when a boss replay is opened from the galaxy map itself (map ~90 MB stays under the battle); releasing the on-screen map needs the owner's decision. Not checked in the browser harness; the patch reproduces and the main script passes `node --check`.
- **v714 — iPhone crash fixes from feedback** (`tools/patches/p714_wire.py`, which runs `p_fb20261121_iospace.py` and `p_fb20261121_memfresh.py`; owner approved releasing branches fb-20261121-1059 and fb-20261121-1221). On iOS a paced scene (map and base) builds at most 2 new shader programs a frame (`r.pace.max`, `B.n`), so the map fills in over a fraction of a second instead of one compile spike that got the tab killed. `worldDrop()` clears `G.memEst`, so the heartbeat right after a 3D world is released measures memory again instead of reporting the released world. - **v691 — Aexion's Warform remastered in the battle replay** (`tools/patches/p691_wire.py` + `p691_aexw.js`; owner: "slow aexions war form turning speed and frequency in battle simulator, also his attacks need to be timed better with his animation"). The Warform (`b.r.sbForm`) used the generic `wbFend` cycle (0.65 s snap turn to a random side every ~6 s) and drew lance/sweep/fracture on the fight clock (`AEX_DUR`, sim seconds) while the model played on real time. Now `aexwQueue`/`aexwDraw` run one real-time beat per attack like the dreadnought: turn only if the target is on another side (2.6 s smootherstep, 3.6 s across), keep that facing afterwards, a 450 ms warning glow, the model move via `bossAct`, the drawn effect remapped onto the model's clock by `AEXW_MAP` (`aexwU`), hold 600 ms, rest 3 s. Measured in the harness: lance strike 1.0 s after the move starts (model release 0.96 s), sweep cut 0.83–0.92 s (blade crosses 0.78–1.35 s), fracture snap 3.64 s (overdrive ends 3.57 s); turns ~2.2–2.6 s, every 15–25 s instead of every ~6 s. Missiles and broadside still draw through `WB_STRIKE.aex` off `WBR.mv`, already on the model's clock. Damage unchanged.
- **v690 — the Advisor's Titan lines in battle retired** (`tools/patches/p690_wire.py`; owner: "I think I want to retire the titans lines in battle simulation"). The v606 opening line (your Titan, or a warning about theirs: `titAdvBattle`) and the call-out on a Titan ultimate (`titAdv("ult")`) no longer play. The ultimate's slow-motion moment stays, and so do the Titan lines outside battle (commissioned, enough Titan Cores). `TADV.battle/foe/ult` are left in place, unused. Verified: a battle with an Armageddon, a Sovereign Ark and an enemy Harbinger opens with no Advisor line.
- **v689 — the orphaned joint strike, the real white light, and a save rollback** (`tools/patches/p689_wire.py`; owner screenshots after v688: "Nothings changed"). Found in the live data: the owner's Cube strike `assaults/a2hrz2xvbq7r` had already fought (21:17 UTC, a draw) and their fleet was home-bound at 21:19, but at 22:38 an older copy of the empire (from before the launch) was saved over the newer one, so fleet f3810 was back in flight to a strike whose record is `done`. That record is not in `G.assaults` (only `open` ones are), so the Fleets tab showed no joint-strike buttons and the fleet would fly on for 14 h. Fixes: (1) `strikeOrphans(o)` runs on every assaults snapshot: a fleet whose strike record is done or missing lands at once and takes the recorded result (or turns home). (2) Fleets heading home from a joint strike (they keep `f.assault`) now get afterburners, boosts, Hurry, the ↩ HOME chip and alliance help. (3) The rollback: a copy that stopped saving (store.paused, another device took over) still stamped `S.cloudAt` on every flush, so on reload it looked in sync and won at sign-in. Now a paused copy doesn't stamp, and `localStorage[KEY+".cseq"]` keeps the save number this device last wrote or loaded; sign-in takes the account's save when its seq is past that. (4) The white light was the warp-trail cosmetic: 15 additive glows dropped every 80 ms where the fleet was, so a fleet crawling across a 19 h march stacked them on one spot. The trail is now laid along the course behind the fleet. (5) `CUBE_FORM.slot` read the flagship's size when there was no flagship: every map frame threw before fleets were drawn (commanders without a flagship flying to the Cube during the event). Verified in the harness: blob gone (comet tail instead), orphan lands and comes home with the draw survivors, home speed-ups work (18.9 h → 12.6 h with afterburners).
- **v688 — speeding up joint strikes everywhere; the white glare on long flights** (`tools/patches/p688_wire.py` + `p688_flight.js`, which replaces the injected p687_home.js block; owner screenshot: a 15-hour joint strike march to the Cube, "still can't speed up", a white glare on the fleet). (1) **Glare**: a fleet's warp flare showed for the first and last 3% of the trip (27 min on a 15 h march, full white at the fleet); `fleetPath` now returns the trip length `d` and the flare lasts 4 s. Verified: off on a 15 h flight 1 min in. (2) **Joint strikes**: they had no speed-up outside the strike's own page and the follow bar (`!f.assault` everywhere). The Fleets tab row of a strike you march in has ⚡ −5 min (boost), ⚡ Afterburners (`strikeBoost` burn), Hurry (`strikeHurry`: `shardCost` of the march left, lands 30 s on) and 🤝 Ask for help. The top-bar chip covers strikes ("⚔ STRIKE", tap = the strike page, ⚡ = its afterburners) as well as fleets home. (3) **Help for any flight**: `fhOK` (out, home, or your strike), ids `s-<strike>` / `<fleet>-<phase>`; help on a strike speeds the whole strike (`strikeCut`, the same march maths as `strikeBoost`). Verified on a simulated 15 h strike: chip, row buttons, afterburners 15 → 10 h, 3 helps 10 → 8.84 h, hurry → 30 s.
- **v687 — speeding fleets home** (`tools/patches/p687_wire.py` + `p687_home.js`; owner picked ideas 1 and 3). Afterburners, Afterburner Fuel, Boosts and Hurry already worked on the way home (`fleetSpeedUp` handles phase "back"). (1) `homeHB`: whenever a fleet is flying home (more than 20 s left), the top bar on the map (before the Cube band) and on the planet (after the Cube call) shows "↩ HOME" with the countdown (the latest-landing fleet; "↩ 2 HOME" for two) and a ⚡ ◆cost chip (afterburners in one tap); tapping the chip follows the fleet (`fleetMap`). (2) Alliance help on the trip home: `a.fhelp[uid] {id: fhId(f), k, e, t, by}` beside `a.help` (kept by `cleanAlly`); 🤝 in the follow bar and on the fleet's row asks (`askFHelp`, one request per member, replaced by the next trip); requests list in the alliance help card with Help (`giveFHelp`, shares HELP_DAY and the rewards of construction help); `fhelpApply` (from `helpApply`) cuts 4% of the time left per help, at least 30 s, up to HELP_MAX; `allyHelpN` counts both kinds for the badges. Verified in the harness: chips on both screens (screenshot), chip follows the fleet, ask shows 🤝 0/10, three helps cut 19:27 → 16:59, the card lists another member's trip with Help, the badge counts it, Help records. The alliance-doc writes were mocked.
- **v686 — the Super Cube falls to one strong assault; the holders can call for reinforcements** (`tools/patches/p686_wire.py`; owner: "I used to be able to take it with me and 30 boys", then "yes" to one assault, and "allow alliance holding the cube to call alliance for additional reinforcements for all alliance members regardless of distance"). (1) `cubeFight`: the rise now aims at a win (frac = 1 on a win, else at most .9; `riseFight` t0 .99), so the Warden grows only as far as this fleet can still destroy it; if the risen fight is not won, the unscaled fight is tried. The owner's alliance fleet of 2026-11-20 now wins at every wear level (rise 9 fresh). A fleet weaker than the Warden itself (F <= 1) still fights it unscaled. (2) **Call for reinforcements**: `cur.call {by, aly, at, until}` on event/nexus (kept by `cleanNexus`); `ACT.nxCall` (any member of the holding alliance, under the event lock, one call at a time, 20 min = `NX_CALL`) posts to the alliance chat; while it is on, `nexusGo` sends the holders' reinforcements as a warp (`sendFleet` extra `{arrive: now + NX_WARP (2 min), tt, warp: 1}`, no fuel); `nxCallBand` shows "📣 THE CUBE CALLS" on the map (omBand) and the planet (HUD strip), tap = the Cube page; the page shows the call button or the active call. Verified in the harness (simulated alliance): button, banner, band, warp 120 s with 0 fuel and an empty tank; the online write path (lock, set, chat) mirrors resolveNexus and was not run live. No new spoken lines.
- **Voice batch 6 (with v685)**: the last 62 unvoiced lines: the 11 Mimic battle lines (AWE_MIM), 47 Archive readings of the Mimic records (frag, interp, adv, enc, def), the Convergence FAQ answer, "The Omega Cube is awake." (DVS) and the two forge lines. Found by a scan of every speech table and literal speech call (`tvoice2.js` in the scratch harness: tables spoken whole, cinema `line` fields, ARCH archFragLine/interp/adv/enc/def, FAQ answers, DVS[4], literals in say/sbSay/advHolo/speak/advNews/bVoice calls); it now finds 0 unvoiced lines. ElevenLabs key "UDF voice batch 6" (TTS only, 6,000-credit cap, 1-day expiry; cleared from the page after use), UDF Advisor v4, eleven_v4, seed 4242, mood tags as moods.py (Mimic awe lines [in awe]/[urgently], archives [gravely]); zipped in the page (store zip), staged from Downloads, conv5.sh, env.json rebuilt (1,750 clips), voice_add.py. Clip lengths checked (6-25 chars/s); three play in the harness.
- **v685 — the memory leak behind the iPhone crashes; the Super Cube within reach again; every boss move crosses the screen; reactive boss shields; Mimic boss shields (sim v9)** (`tools/patches/p685_wire.py` + `p685_mem.js` + `p685_shield.js`). (1) **Leak**: three.js puts a "dispose" listener holding the renderer on every geometry/material/texture a renderer draws; objects shared between renderers (three's own PMREM background box, BORG's blocks, cached sprites) are never disposed, so every released map and base renderer stayed alive with its canvases and buffers. Measured on a simulated iPhone 15 Pro Max (Chromium, real renderer RSS after full GC): old build 297 → 1,043 MB over four map/base round trips and still climbing; new build steady at ~410 MB on the base / ~510 MB on the map; 16 battle replays level at ~440 MB. Fix: `TH.WebGLRenderer` is wrapped so each renderer records the dispose listeners it adds (render, compile, setRenderTarget, initTexture...) and removes them when disposed (glFree, map3DRebuild, t3Free). Heap snapshots confirm the old maps and bases are collected. Note for testing: CDP `Runtime.queryObjects` results keep their objects alive unless released, which looks like a leak. (2) **Super Cube**: `riseFight` scales the Warden so one attack takes a target share of it, but `cubeKilled` counted units, mostly cheap drones, so it rose until only drones died (owner's alliance 2026-11-20: Cube atk 19.4M / hull 259M, 540x the Nov 8 Cube they beat; lost 41 of 68 units, all drones). `cubeKilled` and `wardenWear` now count hull (`cubeHW`). Same alliance fleet: rise 603 → 88, all spheres and drones and one of two Cubes fall (75% of hull), the Warden breaks below half so the next assault takes it. (3) **Strikes**: `WB_STRIKE.scb` (tractor beam, burst bolts, wave), and world-boss bite/coil/charge/summon; `wbStrike` serves the Super Cube (`superSide`, no `sbk`/`omega`). Verified per move with tprobe (cube, dev, ovl, war, wym). (4) **Reactive shields**: every absorbed shot on a boss records `bShHit`; `bShHits` draws a hex ripple where it lands, lights the facing rim, shimmers the bubble under sustained fire (21 hits in Aexion's first 6 s). (5) **Sim v9**: Mimic bosses (`malpha*`, `momega*`, `mnexus`, `mimic`) get the 12 s shield pulse (Omega verified: 60% at 10/22/34 s). The Super Cube gets no pulse (owner: "still way too strong"). settle_fight: accepts v9, sim.js rebundled (CB_V 9), still agrees with the client on the 7 boss fights.
- **v684 — fallen super bosses stay down; phones never render the map below 1x; The Shadow at the Wall; boss checks on the server again** (`tools/patches/p684_wire.py`). Khezaroth and Medusara released their `die` drive when the fall ended (`dd < 1 ? dd : -1`), so the model stood back up beside its glyph while the Advisor said it was gone; now `Math.min(1, dd)` (live and preview; verified in the Lab preview: old build shows Khezaroth at 97 s, new shows only the glyph). `adaptRes` floor on phones at the lightest memory tier was 0.6x (owner's iPhone logged pr 0.6 on a 3x screen: blocky); now at least 1x. om1: the Leviathan swims the wall in ring mode (as on the map since v617, owner wanted it laid out, not coiled), its debris hidden (tinted black they read as black squares), `sweep` only; the event band shows "Happening now" instead of repeating a stage name contained in the event name, on one line. Server: `settle_fight` accepts sim v3–8; `sim.js` rebundled from v683 (it was v529; `fmt` added to the bundle, a boss destroyed outright crashed it); verified 7 boss fights (aex, khz incl. 0 left, wym) agree with the client exactly. Deployed as settle_fight version 5 (2026-11-20) through the Supabase connector: the deployed index.ts imports sim.js from jsDelivr pinned to commit 95997f4 (the connector can't take the 53 KB file reliably); the repo's index.ts still imports ./sim.js for CLI deploys. After a rebundle, commit, then redeploy with the new commit hash. Noted, not fixed: the owner's iPhone closes after 1.2–1.3 s freezes when the map opens (crash logs 2026-11-20), which is what pushed it into the lightest memory tier.
- **v683 — bosses raise their shields again; Aexion's dreadnought form reworked** (`tools/patches/p683_aexd.js`, `p683_wire.py`). Sim **v8** (`CB_V 8`): a boss unit (`/^wb[A-Z]/` in AL: world bosses, super bosses, Omega sections) restores its shield to 60% every 12 s from 10 s, under fire too (before, every boss's shield was gone in 1–3 s and never returned, since recharge needs 2 s unhit). Sections destroyed per fight unchanged within noise (aex 80→78, khz 79→79, omg 78→77, wym 18→17). `bShDraw` flashes a hex bubble when the boss's shield jumps. Dreadnought form (`aexdOn`: sbk aex, sbForm 0): `wbFend` no longer turns it at random; `aexdQueue`/`aexdDraw` run one real-time beat per attack (turn 1.5 s toward the target, or a 0.5 s settle when it is ahead; fire with the model's own move; hold; turn back; rest 2.6 s). Rotation missiles (from spine cells) → lance (rail shot from the bow) → broadside (rippling down the deck); special **Sovereign Rail** (callout, hull drains into the bow, a sweeping beam across the fleet; at most twice, past 30%/70%, signature voice line). Targets skip squadrons tangled with the hull. `WBR.face = -dir` (the model's bow is opposite the face sign). Warform unchanged. Note: `settle_fight` (shadow check) still only accepts sim v3–6 and bundles the v529 sim, so it has rejected boss fights since v673; logging only, no player effect.
- **v682 — the Omega Cube's model follows its battle** (`tools/patches/p682_wire.py`). `BMV_ACT.omg` (tractor→tractor, wave→wave, split→burst); `bossAct` keys on `bmvKey` so Omega fights (`r.omega`, no `sbk`) are included; `wbFend` skips random moves when `bmvKey(battle) === key`. Verified: model moves logged tractor/wave/tractor matching the battle. (Super Cube / world-boss strike extras were drafted but not wanted: branch `draft-v682-extra-bosses`, unmerged.)
- **v681 — Khezaroth, Medusara, Vaelthar and the Omega Cube attack across the screen; boss models play the battle's moves; Aexion's missiles reach the ships; boss voice lines** (`tools/patches/p681_boss.js`, `p681_wire.py`). `BMV` gives each boss a two-move rotation plus a signature (khz seals/crush + nova, med gaze/serpents + shards, vae rain/halo + wheel, omg tractor/wave + split; signature at most twice, past 30% and 70% of the fight), drawn by `bmvDraw` from the boss to the ships with `aexBoom` impacts. `bossAct` makes the boss's 3D model play the matching move (`BMV_ACT`); `wbFend` no longer fires random model moves in those fights. `wbStrike` now runs for super bosses too, and `WB_STRIKE.aex` adds missiles/broadside, which Aexion's rotation now includes (lance, missiles, sweep, broadside): before, his missiles flew inside his picture box and stopped at its edge. `bVoice` speaks an opening line and a signature line per fight through `awePick`/`sbSay`; `AWE_MIM` adds Mimic lines (captions only until recorded). Verified in the clock harness: moves alternate, the model follows, missiles land on the ship lines.
- **v680 — Aexion fights like a swordsman (Nov 20)** (p680_wire.py + p680_aex.js). His replay shots used the generic capital beam. Now `aexAtkAdd` (from spawnVolley, sbk "aex") schedules moves on his real volleys, alternating lance and sweep with a gap of the move's length + 0.7 s; damage is unchanged.
  - **Lance:** a charged thrust whose beam runs past the target to beyond the screen, with after-images; delayed booms on ships along the line (`aexBoom` inserts into b.pend in time order).
  - **Sweep:** a blade of light swung from his body across the whole foe arc, with cut marks and staggered booms.
  - **Time fracture (`frac`):** at most twice, after 30% and 70% of b.end. A cold dim overlay (the canvas "saturation" blend washed the screen white in Chromium, so it is not used), slow motion, 3 ghosts (WBR.soft art) striking, then a snap flash and booms.
  - **Parries (`b.aexP`):** a hex shield and a glance when a capital, titan or Cube Destroyer volley targets him.
  - **Warform entrance:** a burst during the intro when sbForm is 1; the banner name comes via b.heroN.
  - **Director and sound:** cineDirect frames the lance between him and the target, and goes wide for sweep and frac. aexSfx reuses mimSfx/SFX.
  - **Verified:** stress fights aex sbForm 0/1 at iPhone size with tcine.js (SEEK to reach the fracture); fracture brightness measured at 70–79 vs 80–95 normal.
- **v679 — No letterbox bars (Nov 19)** (p679_wire.py). The v678 bars were drawn at mg.top/mg.bot (the ships' field), not the visible edges; on the owner's Fold they cut across the middle of the battle. Bars removed, grain kept. Seen while checking: a long flat green bar across a Light Fighter badge in a Super Cube fight in the harness, present with cinematic off too (pre-existing, not in the owner's screenshot); not yet traced.
- **v678 — Cinematic replays; Mimics sway slower (Nov 19)** (p678_wire.py + p678_cine.js). Owner: "slow the battle simulation down in a cinematic style but keep the same damage; movie finish". Visual only: the battle record, losses and result are untouched.
  - **Mode:** `b.cine` (default on, localStorage udf.cineRep; 🎞/⚡ button, `ACT.bcine`).
  - **Pace:** `CINE_PACE` .62 on the replay clock (with slowUntil as before). An 8-round battle now takes about 3 min to watch at 1×; 2×/4× remain.
  - **Rhythm:** `cineDue` puts volleys on a 0.2 s beat, side d on the off-beat (≤0.2 s of delay, visual only).
  - **Director (`cineDirect`, auto camera only):** opening shots of each fleet during a ≥3 s intro (`cineIntro`); then Mimic big attacks (storm/omega/grab/slam/claw/lance; omegaX gets a wide 3.4 s hold), Cube Destroyer/Titan beams, the heaviest salvo within 1.1 s, and a wide shot every third cut; the winner at the end. Focus uses `b.lastP`, and the camera eases over 950 ms.
  - **Film look (`cineLook`):** letterbox bars (wider at opening and finish) and overlay grain, skipped on low FX.
  - **Fixes:** "ASSIMILATED" is drawn directly (replays clear b.nums since v603). Mimics' battle loop (`monBatFrame`) runs at 0.5× (owner: slower reads more real).
  - **Verified:** clips via `$S/gt/tcine.js` with Playwright's clock (install + pauseAt + runFor 1/15 s): CD attack, Cube assault, Omega phase 1. Pace measured at 0.216 battle-s per s.
- **v677 — Mimics without legs; cinematic attacks (Nov 19)** (p677_wire.py + p677_mimfx.js; the old v676 attack code is cut out and replaced). Owner: "remove their legs and walking animations and just make their tentacles move; cooler attack animations."
  - **Body:** buildMimic's limbs are replaced by 10–16 hanging tentacles (`D.legs` now sets how many). mimWave's trot becomes a float bob with the lower tentacles swaying, and the same wave runs on the map and in battle frames.
  - **Tentacle attacks (`MIM_TENT`, `mimTent`):** bite 1, grab 3, claw 2, storm 3. A tapered dark cable with a lit rim and suckers reaches, spears, holds and retracts. The prey is yanked toward the attacker (never bosses); a prey that dies is dragged all the way in (devour, `m.dev`).
  - **Impact (`hit`):** hit-stop via b.slowUntil (150–260 ms; 900 ms for Omega's signature), shake, flash and a shockwave ring.
  - **Per-kind effects:** acid spit has a drip trail, a splash and 3 s burns (`b.mBurn`). The assimilation link crawls circuitry over the prey and shows "ASSIMILATED" if it dies (b.nums). The Alphas' `storm` adds a roar ring and chain lightning through up to 3 other foes (`mimBolt`). Omega's signature `omegaX` fires at most twice a fight (the first omega volley and one after half time): the screen darkens and light drains into the core, then a beam sweeps from the leftmost to the rightmost enemy.
  - **Sound (`mimSfx`):** whip, hiss and hum are synthesised on the battle bus (SFX.bv); slam uses SFX.blast; Omega uses SFX.charge then megaBeam.
  - **Verified:** Lab fights om1, alpha2 and hive3 at iPhone size, with clips made by stepping simT while paused (`$S/gt/tclip.js`). Attacks seen: spit, omegaX, storm, omega, bite, slam, link, grab.
- **v676 — Mimics animate in battle, each kind with its own attack (Nov 19)** (p676_wire.py + p676_mimatk.js). Owner: "I thought the mimics were supposed to move anatomically?" Their motion (`mimWave` → `MON_WAVE`) was already baked into battle frames, but drawBattle only asked `monBatFrame` for `MON` creatures, so Mimics were stills.
  - **Frames:** now `MIM_D` kinds animate too. `mimAnimOK` limits this to the biggest kinds by `MIM_HULL`: 3 on iOS/WEAK, 6 elsewhere (10–16 frames each, a memory guard).
  - **Attacks:** `MIM_ATK` per kind (bite, spit, grab, link, slam, claw, lash, pulse, brood, lance, omega) starts from `spawnVolley` (`mimAtkAdd`, one at a time per squadron, timed to the real volley). The body lunges or rears (`mimLunge`, applied to `scr` after layout) and the effect draws after wbStrike (`mimAtkDraw`). Omega's lance shakes and flashes the screen once.
  - Verified in the Lab: hive 4 / Alpha II / Omega phase 1 fights show bite, spit, link, slam, lash and omega attacks, with frames ready for every kind. In the software-GL harness a hive-4 opening took up to 120 s to bake; until frames are ready the old stills are drawn, so nothing waits on them.
- **v675 — Map fleet size measurement fix; Finder drops "Fleets in flight" (Nov 19)** (p675_wire.py).
  - **Owner's iPhone:** a returning fleet showed as a huge, smeared red flagship in a white blow-out. v674's 30 px minimum size read the screen size from `pxS` (built from `W.size`, which adaptRes zeroes to force a resize) and misjudged it in normal frames as well. In the harness the same fleet drew at 1.6× up close and 3.4× zoomed out (scale 0.34 / 0.72 instead of 0.21). It now measures from the canvas's clientHeight, ignores a zero reading, and boosts at most 4×. Verified: the scale holds at 0.213 at every zoom while W.size is zeroed every 50 ms. **Not proven** as the sole cause of the smear: the v674 build stayed finite in the same test. If it recurs, look at the flagship look aura and LOD at close range.
  - **Finder:** "Fleets in flight" no longer shows in the Finder (sub.gal "targets"). The Mine tab still lists them.
- **v674 — Map fleets: one flagship per fleet, sized against the planets (Nov 19)** (p674_wire.py + p674_fleets.js). Owner (iPhone screenshot): fleets were 2–5 planets long, the Cube Destroyer was an orb at each fleet's nose, and five attacks on one Cube piled into a blob. Measured at iPhone size: planets 77–98 px across, fleets 120–490 px.
  - **Only the flagship (owner's call):** each map fleet is one hull (`FLEET_SOLO`, `fleetSolo`): the faction flagship, or the fleet's biggest ship when it has none. A Cube Destroyer, when carried, flies beside it. Joint strikes keep their armada. Battle replays and map clashes keep full fleets.
  - **Armada bug:** fleet3D's `big` argument doubles as the formation name, so every ordinary fleet (formation "wedge", a string) was drawn as a joint-strike armada (×3 hulls per class, ×2 count). `FLEET_ARM` now marks real armadas.
  - **Size by strength:** `fleetSzOf` sets the formation span (`userData.span`) to `FLEET_SPAN` [1.05, 1.35, 1.7, 2.1] × `PL_D` (a typical planet diameter) by `fleetTier`: unit cost thresholds 5e5 / 2e7 / 5e8, with any titan at least tier 2 and a Cube Destroyer tier 3. Joint strikes multiply that, up to 2.6. A fleet in flight never drops below 30 px on screen.
  - **Formation:** the Cube Destroyer sorts last, at the rear.
  - **Lanes:** `fleetLanes` (W.lanes): outbound fleets to the same pid fan out sideways by 1.15× their span as they close in.
  - Verified at 393×852 (iPhone): 5 fleets to one world show as 5 single ships on separate lanes, with Cube Destroyer fleets as flagship + Cube Destroyer.
- **v673 — Mimics sized like monsters, Cube Destroyer fires from the start and no longer slides, Archive replay (Nov 19)** (p673_wire.py + p673_scale.js).
  - **Mimic size:** they had no entry in the size ladder, so every Mimic fell back to the default hull length. Omega was drawn at 1.89 (a Titan is 3.2, the Cube Destroyer 4.3) and the Alphas below a Dreadnought. `MIM_HULL` puts them on the ladder (Omega 5.4/5.8, Alphas 3.9, Nexus 3.2, Brood Titan 2.7). `bCapW` lets heroes span 78% of the screen. `bFitL` caps every hull at 95% of the screen width; a lone Cube Destroyer whose foes had died could reach 150%. Echoes (`e_*`) copy their hull's length. `DRAWK` is larger for the mostly-empty Pylon, Node and Nexus sprites, and `MIM_STA` gives the Mimic stations (Nexus, Node, Pylon, Hive nest) their own larger station size. The swarm (`MIM_PACK`) draws up to 5 bodies.
  - **Hero opening:** units of length 3.6 or more get a 2.7 s opening (`bIntroMs`). They rise from 55% to full size (`bRise`, `b.intro0`), and the banner names them (`b.heroN`).
  - **Coverage test** (the `$S/gt/tcov.js` harness, using the `b.lastP` hook), largest Mimic as a share of screen width: Omega 37% → 86–95%, Alphas 20–34% → 55–92%, Brood Titan 24% → 49%. The Mimic side's share of its half went from 3–15% to 12–29%.
  - **Cube Destroyer slide:** it was drawn first at its raw simulation spot and glided 55–100 px into its post. It is now locked to its post (lay ease 1) from the first frame.
  - **Cube Destroyer firing:** combat **v7**. The Cube Destroyer's beam lances have siege range (`rngOf`, 160). In v6 it crawled from the rear at speed 1.2 and first fired about 15–17 s in (about 45 s of replay at BATTLE_PACE .35). The bigger bug: `sim.v` was written with a hard-coded list that stopped at 6, so every replay re-ran as v6. CB_OK accepts 7; v6 replays are unchanged. The first shot now comes at 0.3 s, with 19 shots per 40 s battle instead of 12. Merged Cube Destroyer formations (`b.repOf`) fire from the drawn ship's gun port and turn it. **Balance:** Cube Destroyers now deal damage from round 1. **Server:** `supabase/functions/settle_fight/sim.js` is still the v529 bundle (checks off); re-run tools/sim_bundle.py before turning settle_fight on.
  - **Archive play / stop / play:** advHolo dropped any line said in the last 2 minutes, which swallowed every line of a replayed record. On top of that, the first line waited up to 60 s for ambient chatter to clear. Now play stops her voice and reads at once (`advHolo(l, "arch")` bypasses freshness), and other Advisor lines hold off while `G.archRd` is set.
  - Verified: all 16 Lab fights, 45/45 stress tests pass (v7), Cube Destroyer drawn equal to its post from the first frame, Archive play → stop → play → stop → play reads the record each time.

Rows with `t: "mim"` now take their name from `AL`. Verified on the stress screen: 45 rows, all passed, ▶ replay of Omega plays.

Stress tests gain 8 Mimic cases (`t: "mim"`) with their escorts; cases can carry `mk` (defender maker) and `d0` (full defender set), and `stressCheck` compares against `d0` so escorts are not flagged as "grew". When the fifth seal weakens, the swarm wakes."). `finWinOf` for typ "mim" = the Mimic finale weekend of that season (Season 7, index PRO+4+10n: VP start + 26 days, 2 days); `finOutcome` holds the seal when Omega died in that run (`o.s === s0 && o.dead`); back in `finKeys`, so the ledger and the Eclipse verdict count ten seals again; the season-finale card shows "Chapter 5 · Omega Awakens" (`seaFinHTML` b defaults to krk). The record c_5 rewritten to match (art/mimic/nexus.webp); FIN_SAY.krk emptied (her Kraken lines retired). **Archives: a new category "The Mimic Protocol"** with six records (m_protocol, m_alpha, m_echo, m_vault, m_omega, m_frag; `archImg`/`archName`/testament filter take the m_ prefix) unlocked in play: first hive destroyed (protocol), first Alpha or Nexus fight and an Alpha kill (alpha), an Echo made and wiped (echo), the arrival cinematic and phase 2 (vault), first Omega attack and the end (omega), the final transmission (fragment).
